Title: Warnings of the Eternal Spirit, by the mouth of his servant John, sirnam'd Lacy; the second part
Creator: John Lacy
Publisher: London: printed for B. Bragg (imprint partly legible in the scan)
Publication Date: 1707
Rights: Public Domain (published 1707, more than 95 years ago; public domain by date under the project's rolling rule; the item carries no rights field, so the date rule alone applies)
Source: Internet Archive item bim_eighteenth-century_warnings-of-the-eternal-_lacy-john-pseudo-proph_1707 (https://archive.org/details/bim_eighteenth-century_warnings-of-the-eternal-_lacy-john-pseudo-proph_1707), the item's own bim_eighteenth-century_warnings-of-the-eternal-_lacy-john-pseudo-proph_1707_djvu.txt full-text OCR derivative; contributing library per the item metadata: Internet Archive; host metadata date: 1707. Fetched directly (curl/urllib, raw bytes) by the Library thread (Era VIII pass), 2026-10-23. sha256 of the raw fetched bytes: bbfec3cd69eeb6306b16f34ac561833e8e4fcda7a9161e0cbe2eb513831cb308.
Provenance note: Header prepended by the Library thread; nothing else altered, no OCR error corrected, nothing retyped. British Library microfilm scan (Eighteenth Century Collections Online); title page garbled, the warnings readable with the long s kept.
Content note: The inspired warnings of John Lacy, the English gentleman who joined the French Prophets in London, with accounts of the agitations and gifts in the London meetings; the Camisard inspiration as it passed to English followers.

XXXVI. The ſame Day. B. Ef. 
It ĩs for you to truſt in my Words, and wait 
for the fulfilling of them. The Experience of your 
Faith in Expettation will yield you Satisfaction; and . 
I try you that I may know the Truth of your Belief. 
'Tis not becoming you to haften, and be impatient 
under my ſeeming Delay, which is not uch. Thoſe 
that ſee but a few Links of the Chain, a ſmall piece 
of the Workmanſhip, know not how much the ſeem-* 
ng DEL CINE to the Beauty of the Work; 
how neceſſary to the End, which is to be the Manifeſ- 
tation of all my Glory to this World. You have 2

trodutory, Leave me to compoſe the Harmony of 
all things, and all my Meaſures every where. My gra-

of you. They will leave the Oppoſers more excuſe- 
leſs, and demonſtrate the Equity of the Diſpoſer.

LXIV. Thurſday, August 14. B. Ey. F.

troſpect: but begin thou, before this Week expires!
